- New lesson L-044 added on the hedge-acknowledgment-without-engagement pattern: when a Polish tick closes a Verify P1 about hedging an open question (e.g., PI-007’s casebook-acknowledgment clause closing the surface gap), the immediate next Verify pass often surfaces a deeper P1 about whether the hedge engages with the hedge’s architectural implications elsewhere in the article rather than only naming the open question. The fix is to require any Polish closure of a hedge-naming P1 to also add one sentence engaging with the architectural consequence of the hedged fact, with a cross-reference to the section that develops the consequence. The PI-007-and-PI-063 pair is the canonical example: PI-007’s hedge closed the casebook-silence surface, PI-063 surfaced the architectural-implication gap the hedge opened by naming the question without engaging with what the answer would mean for the curriculum-grounding architectural claim. The lesson is distinct from L-024 (Cite-time transfer-of-inference clause for citation-density patterns) because L-044 is a Polish-time prose-level engagement requirement, not a Cite-time citation-density requirement. LESSONS.md is at 43 of the 50-entry cap before this entry; after L-044 lands the cap is at 44.

## Run 2026-05-23T01:00:00Z

Read substrate: `manuscript/full-draft.md` (7,744 words; eight sections present in order II → III → IV → V → VI → VII → VIII → IX; six sections plus the abstract marked PENDING). Section II’s 745 prose words are the new substance since the prior Verify tick, landed by the 2026-05-22T21:00 Polish tick and assembled by the 2026-05-22T23:00 Stitch tick. Sections III through IX are unchanged at the body-prose level since the prior Verify run; their forty-one open P1 findings remain routed for the next Polish and Cite ticks on those sections. Each persona reads the full assembled draft independently and attacks Section II as the primary new surface, the II-to-III seam at the new top of the assembled article as a candidate Stitch-consistency observation that the prior Stitch narrative flagged, and the cross-section recurrent-pattern surface for any pattern that now appears at five-or-more sites. P0 reserved for desk-reject issues; nothing in Section II rose to that severity, so all forced findings landed at P1.

### Persona 1: Skeptical JLE Editor

- [P1] Section II.B, paragraph 1, first sentence — “a flat folder of more than two hundred sixty files exported from a law-school course management system: sixty-one PowerPoint lecture decks, sixty-six PDFs of case readings, a dozen merged reading packets, several practice exams, and a small set of teaching guides, organized only by filename.” The colon-introduced enumeration reads as exhaustive of the “more than two hundred sixty files” count, but the listed buckets sum to roughly one hundred forty items (61 + 66 + 12 + a small remainder), well under half the stated total. The 260-plus figure reconciles only when the Uploaded Media subfolder’s one hundred thirty-five individual case-opinion PDFs (which Section IV.B itemizes) are added back in, but Section II.B does not flag the subfolder, so a peer reviewer doing the obvious arithmetic against the colon-list will catch the gap before reaching Section IV. A JLE editor will treat the apparent numerical inconsistency as a credibility-puncturing finding within the article’s first quantitative claim.
  Suggested fix: in the next Polish pass on Section 02, rewrite II.B’s first sentence so the colon-list is named as top-level categories rather than as exhaustive of the count; for example, “a flat folder of more than two hundred sixty files exported from a law-school course management system, with the top level holding sixty-one PowerPoint lecture decks, sixty-six PDFs of case readings, a dozen merged reading packets, several practice exams, and a small set of teaching guides, all organized only by filename, and an Uploaded Media subfolder carrying the bulk of the individual case-opinion PDFs the top-level set indexed.” The rewrite preserves the 260-plus aggregate, names the subfolder, and matches Section IV.B’s itemized accounting without duplicating it.
  Routing: Section 02 polish_status → needs_polish.

- [P1] Section II.E, paragraph 1, first and second sentences — “the article sits within an established Journal of Legal Education line of pedagogical-reform pieces that adapt inherited teaching forms instead of rejecting them … the rhetorical posture this article adopts toward ordinary course infrastructure parallels the posture Abrams adopts toward Socratic teaching.” Two consecutive self-flagging-meta-rhetoric constructions in the same closing paragraph: the first announces the article’s venue placement (“the article sits within”) and the second announces the article’s rhetorical posture (“the rhetorical posture this article adopts”). This is the fifth section in which the article frames a structural move with explicit-significance scaffolding rather than letting the move read itself, recurring the PI-022 (VIII.E), PI-028 (III.E), PI-036 (VI.B), and PI-060 (IV.E) pattern L-036 named. The Section 02 Polish tick at 2026-05-22T21:00 ran with L-036 in its declared lesson scope and these constructions still landed in published prose, confirming the PI-060 observation that the L-036 sweep is not catching the category at Polish time across multiple ticks. A JLE editor reading II.E will note the press-release scaffolding the same way the prior four instances were flagged.
  Suggested fix: in the next Polish pass on Section 02, rewrite II.E’s opening two sentences so the venue-placement and posture-parallel moves are made rather than announced; for example, “The course knowledge system extends the casebook, the syllabus, and the live classroom rather than displacing any of them. Jamie R. Abrams’s reframing of the Socratic method belongs to a Journal of Legal Education line of pedagogical-reform pieces that adapt inherited teaching forms instead of rejecting them, and the same move applies here: the inherited form is not the deficiency the proposal is built to remedy, and the contribution is the layer the inherited form was never designed to carry.” The rewrite preserves the Abrams parallel and the venue-fit signal without the “sits within” and “rhetorical posture this article adopts” scaffolding.
  Routing: Section 02 polish_status → needs_polish.

- [P2] Section II.D, paragraph 2, third sentence — “The deployed vault carries an explicit wiki-link grammar in which Topic pages link to their cases and lectures …” The phrase “the deployed vault” conflates the Obsidian vault source (where wiki-link grammar lives) with the deployed static site (where wiki-links resolve to HTML hyperlinks at build time). Footnote `[^7]` correctly distinguishes the wiki-link convention from the deployed navigation surface; the body-prose slip is mechanical. Not actionable as a P1 because the footnote handles the distinction and the substance reads correctly to anyone who reads the footnote, but a careful reader of the body alone will catch the conflation. Flag for the next Polish tick on Section 02 to replace “the deployed vault” with “the vault” in II.D paragraph 2, third sentence.

- [P2] Section II.E, footnote `[^8]` — Abrams 2015 is described as “a recent representative of that line.” Eleven years is on the edge of what reads as recent in a JLE pedagogical-reform survey. Not actionable as a P1 because the underlying use is a positional citation rather than an empirical-currency claim, but the “recent” qualifier may not survive editorial review. Mechanical fix: drop “recent” or replace with “notable” / “well-known” in the next Polish tick on Section 02.

### Persona 2: Legal Pedagogy Traditionalist

- [P1] Section II.D, paragraph 1, second sentence and paragraph 2, closing sentence — “A casebook organizes by reading order, a syllabus by week, and an LMS by post date and folder, and none of those produces the per-doctrine synthesis view the Topic page is.” And: “The navigation surface that grammar produces is the doctrinal map the inherited inventory was never built to make, because none of casebook, syllabus, LMS, or slide deck is a graph.” The two sentences together reduce the casebook to a reading-order sequence and deny that it carries any doctrinal organization, but a modern Con Law casebook is organized doctrinally: chapters move from judicial review through federalism through separation of powers through individual rights, with case sequences chosen to develop a doctrinal arc within each chapter. The casebook is in fact a doctrinal map of sorts, just one organized analytically through case-method progression rather than as a hypertext graph. Recurs the PI-030 pattern Persona 2 named at Section III.B (“the casebook table of contents does not give” doctrine-first entry when most Con Law I casebooks organize their TOCs doctrinally), now extending the same overclaim to Section II’s positioning argument. A Civ Pro traditionalist will note that the article’s framing in II.D treats the casebook as a sequential reading list with no doctrinal organization, which is the kind of claim the inherited form does not deserve and which weakens rather than strengthens the article’s extension-not-displacement thesis.
  Suggested fix: in the next Polish pass on Section 02, rewrite II.D’s casebook framing so the casebook’s analytical-doctrinal organization is acknowledged before the article claims what the casebook does not produce; for example, “A casebook organizes the doctrine through chapter sequences and the case method, a syllabus orders that material by week, and an LMS posts the resulting artifacts by date and folder, but none of those forms produces the per-doctrine synthesis page the Topic page is.” And at the graph claim: “The wiki-link grammar produces a doctrinal map at the navigation surface, an affordance the casebook, syllabus, LMS, and slide deck were not built to deliver because none of those is a hypertext graph.” The rewrites preserve the architectural contrast while granting the casebook the doctrinal organization it actually has.
  Routing: Section 02 polish_status → needs_polish.

- [P1] Section II.E-to-III.A seam — Section II.E closes on the venue-positioning and rhetorical-posture-parallel move with Abrams: “the contribution is the layer the inherited form was never designed to carry.” Section III.A opens with a student-eye walkthrough of the deployed shell: “A student opens the deployed case study at https://constitutionallaw.netlify.app and meets a fixed shell.” The prior Stitch tick’s narrative anticipated the seam as “carries from positioning move into case-study altitude without abrupt change of register,” logging it as a candidate Verify observation; reading the seam at full draft confirms the abrupt register shift. II.E’s close is venue-positional and methodological; III.A’s open is a screenshot-grounded user-eye walkthrough. The shift from positioning argument to user experience is exactly the seam pattern PI-062 named at IV.E-to-V.A in the prior Verify run, where each side reads internally clean but the seam carries no substantive bridge. The pattern is now two-for-two across the present partial assembly, which is the kind of recurrence that signals the Stitch consistency check’s seam-criterion needs a “substantive idea across the seam” test rather than only the per-side-clean check it runs.
  Suggested fix: in the next Polish pass on Section 02 (or Section 03), add a one-sentence bridge across the seam. At II.E, the lighter-touch option is to follow the Abrams-parallel close with one new closing sentence such as, “Section III walks the deployed case study at the user-experience altitude before the article returns to the architecture in Section IV.” At III.A, the alternative is to replace the cold open with a transition-first sentence such as, “Section II positioned the course knowledge system as the layer the casebook never carried; this section walks a student through the deployed case study where that layer is the surface the reader meets first.” The III.A version is the lighter-touch fix because Section 02’s Polish queue already carries P1 routings from this run and a transition-first sentence in III.A’s open folds cleanly into Section 03’s existing polish_status: needs_polish.
  Routing: Section 02 polish_status (already routed `needs_polish` this run); Section 03 polish_status (already `needs_polish` from prior Verify rotation).

- [P2] Section II.E, paragraph 1, closing clause — “the contribution is the layer the inherited form was never designed to carry” closely echoes II.A’s closing clause “a course knowledge system is the layer the casebook never carried and was never designed to be.” The repetition gives the section a frame but the language is so close it reads as an unintended near-repeat rather than a deliberate frame. Not actionable as a P1 because the rhetorical-frame move is legitimate, but the next Polish tick on Section 02 should vary the second instance to make the frame deliberate rather than incidental.

### Persona 3: AI-in-Education Researcher

- [P1] Section II (the section as a whole) — Section II is silent on equity, accessibility, device assumptions, and the digital divide as those concerns affect access to course infrastructure both old and new. The section describes a flat-folder LMS export and contrasts it with a course knowledge system, but never acknowledges that LMS-based course infrastructure presupposes broadband-enabled, institutionally-enrolled, sighted, mouse-and-keyboard students, and the deployed course knowledge system inherits every one of those assumptions. The pattern recurs PI-026 (Section VIII), PI-033 (Section III), and PI-064 (Section IV); Section II is now the fourth consecutive flagship walkthrough or positioning section in the article silent on accessibility and equity. An AI-in-higher-education reviewer trained on the equity literature will treat the four-section cumulative silence as a structural literature-engagement gap, and the recurrence pattern means the next Polish tick on any newly-landing section (I, X, XI, XII, XIII, XIV, abstract) is at high risk of repeating the silence by default. Section II is the structurally right place to first acknowledge the access assumption because it is the section that frames what ordinary course infrastructure does and does not produce.
  Suggested fix: in the next Polish pass on Section 02, add one sentence to II.B (or to the II.E positioning paragraph) acknowledging the access assumptions; for example, after II.B’s coexistence-of-two-corpora sentence, add “Both corpora presuppose Canvas-enrolled students with broadband access and devices capable of rendering the modernized PDFs and the deployed wiki at full fidelity; Section XII takes up the equity and access implications of the architectural choice.” The Section XII forward-reference matches the convention the article uses elsewhere for deferring substantive equity work to the section that owns it, and the new sentence breaks the four-section silence pattern without duplicating the substantive argument.
  Routing: Section 02 polish_status → needs_polish.

- [P1] Section II.E, paragraph 1, first sentence and footnote `[^8]` — Section II positions the article within “an established Journal of Legal Education line of pedagogical-reform pieces” and anchors that positioning on Abrams 2015 alone. The section never engages the AI-in-higher-education literature that PERSONAS.md flags as essential (Bond et al. 2024 meta-review on AI in higher education, Kuhail et al. 2023 chatbot-tutor survey, the broader knowledge-graph-RAG and curriculum-grounded-assistant literature that Sections V.D, VIII.E, and IV.A’s footnote chain anchor at one citation remove). Section II is the structurally right place to locate the article in the AI-in-Ed literature because II.D’s “the deployed vault carries an explicit wiki-link grammar” and II.D’s graph-architectural framing are both moves whose pedagogical-effectiveness premises ride on that literature, and the body-prose anchor on legal-pedagogy reform alone leaves the AI-in-Ed reviewer wondering whether the article knows that other architectural choices exist for the same problem. The citation-density pattern Persona 3 named at PI-025 (VIII.E), PI-032 (III.E), PI-040 (VI), and PI-048 (VII) now extends to Section II’s positioning move; Section II is the fifth section in which the AI-in-Ed literature engagement is one-citation-removed or absent.
  Suggested fix: route to the next Cite tick on Section 02 per L-024 (Cite owns load-bearing transfer framing and citation-density end-to-end when external-literature anchoring is the load-bearing move). The future multi-section Cite pass that resolves PI-025, PI-032, PI-040, and PI-048 should consider whether Bond et al. 2024 (meta-review of AI in higher education) and Kuhail et al. 2023 (chatbot-tutor survey) propagate into Section II’s footnote chain at either `[^7]` (where the architectural-contrast wiki-link claim sits and the chatbot-tutor alternative is the natural AI-in-Ed parallel) or `[^8]` (where the venue-fit and pedagogical-reform-line positioning sits), with the L-024 transfer-of-inference clause naming what transfers from each cited intervention and what does not.
  Routing: Section 02 cite_status → needs_cite.

- [P2] Section II.D, paragraph 2, first sentence — “The deployed vault carries an explicit wiki-link grammar in which Topic pages link to their cases and lectures, cases link back to topics and forward to citing cases, and lectures link to the cases and topics they covered.” The description of the wiki-link grammar as a “graph” is correct at the navigational-affordance level but the AI-in-Ed framing slips toward “knowledge graph” territory PERSONAS.md warns about (“misuse or overuse of ‘knowledge graph’ terminology”). The body prose stops short of “knowledge graph” but II.D’s closing sentence “none of casebook, syllabus, LMS, or slide deck is a graph” reads as if any-graph-is-a-knowledge-graph. Not actionable as a P1 because the body avoids the “knowledge graph” phrase, but the Cite tick that lands the AI-in-Ed citation cluster should ensure Section II’s graph framing is anchored to the navigational-affordance literature (the wiki-link / hypertext-doctoral-pedagogy line) rather than the knowledge-graph-RAG literature Section V.D anchors.

It sits in direct tension with Section XI.A's load-bearing disclaimer that the article "does not claim that students learned more ... or that any measurable change ... can be attributed to the deployed vault." ### Persona 2: Legal Pedagogy Traditionalist
- [P1] Section I.B, paragraph 1 ("The essential feature is iteration under the control of a human expert, who reviews, corrects, and signs off on every layer the system produces ... the professor inspects what the system has drafted, corrects what is wrong, and decides whether the work advances") — the introduction defines the expert's contribution entirely as downstream review of AI-drafted output (inspect, correct, sign off, gate), with no mention in the intro that the professor's prior pedagogical judgment about what to teach, how to sequence it, and where students struggle is what shapes the material the system produces (the prompts-as-pedagogical-commitments argument the article does make later at Section VI). A traditionalist reads the intro as reducing the teacher to an editorial quality-gate over generated drafts, the editor-versus-teacher conflation PERSONAS.md warns against; this is a different location and different conflation than PI-078's live-pedagogy-as-loss framing in I.A. (PI-116)
  Suggested fix: in the next Polish tick on Section 01, add one clause to I.B noting that the professor's pedagogical judgment shapes what the system is asked to produce (a forward-reference to Section VI's prompting-as-pedagogical-design), not only what it is asked to fix, so the intro credits teaching expertise upstream and not only editorial sign-off downstream.

### Persona 3: AI-in-Education Researcher
- [P1] Section I.B, paragraph 1, and the thesis restatement ("retrieval from verified legal sources") — naming "retrieval" as one of the five method ingredients, with no qualifier in the intro, reads to a learning-sciences or AI reviewer as runtime retrieval-augmented generation (RAG), the dominant meaning of "retrieval" in this literature. The article's own architecture disavows exactly that: Section VIII.E states "No LLM sits in the request path" and Section XII.B states a reviewed static website "has no runtime generation surface," while Section V.D is the only place that defines the term's intended sense (a manually maintained, professor-supervised "cousin of GraphRAG" where the human-curated link graph does the retrieval work). The intro asserts the RAG-coded term before the article narrows it, inviting the precise misreading the static-architecture argument later spends two sections refuting. (PI-117)
  Suggested fix: in the next Polish tick on Section 01, qualify the intro's "retrieval from verified legal sources" to signal build-time source-linking and verification rather than runtime retrieval (for example "build-time linking to verified legal sources" or a half-clause cross-referencing the Section V.D GraphRAG-cousin framing), so a learning-sciences reader is not primed to read the system as RAG.

### Persona 1: Skeptical JLE Editor
- [P1] Section VI.C paragraphs 3-6 — the body prose recites internal rubric mechanics and prompt constants as if the reader holds the vault config: criterion names (*scope_discipline*, *exam_readiness*), the *exam_readiness* 0.22 weight, scoring rules ("a fact-pattern-only hypo scores zero"; "scoring 3 for one missing and 1 for both missing"), and exact prompt parameters ("roughly 150 words"; "five hypos in a fixed distribution (two same-side, two opposite-side, one fence-sitter)"). The cumulative effect reads as internal system documentation rather than legal scholarship, and the constants are unverifiable from the text (they live in vault rubric files routed to App. A, with the prompt-text appendix App. B not yet assembled, PI-035). Distinct from PI-036 (VI.B self-announcing meta-rhetoric) and PI-040 (single-survey citation density). (PI-137)
  Suggested fix: in the next Polish tick on Section 06, move the bare numeric weight (*exam_readiness* 0.22) and the scoring-rule clauses into footnotes [^5]-[^8] (which already carry them), and keep in the body only the design claim each constant encodes, for example "the rubric penalizes a brief finished in a single pass" without the criterion name and weight, so VI.C argues the pedagogical commitment rather than transcribing the rubric.

### Persona 2: Legal Pedagogy Traditionalist
- [P1] Section VI.C paragraphs 4-5 ("The distributional commitment encodes the law-school exam pedagogy of testing a rule against varying fact patterns at the prompt level" and "encodes the field's interpretive pluralism at the structural level") — equating a fixed five-hypothetical quota (two same-side, two opposite-side, one fence-sitter) and a mandatory one-progressive-one-originalist critique split with "exam pedagogy" and "interpretive pluralism" flattens exam-design judgment into a rigid template. The number, type, and difficulty of hypotheticals a professor sets vary with the doctrine and with what students are getting wrong this year; a fixed quota can produce formulaic, doctrine-insensitive hypos and a false symmetry where the live doctrine has no balanced interpretive divide. Same live-pedagogy-versus-fixed-structure family as PI-031/PI-039/PI-046; distinct from PI-038 (VI.B analytical-work equivalence) and PI-039 (VI.D personas-as-moot-court). (PI-138)
  Suggested fix: in the next Polish tick on Section 06, qualify the two "encodes ... at the [prompt/structural] level" sentences so the fixed distribution operationalizes one exam-pedagogy heuristic (vary fact patterns against the rule) rather than the whole of exam pedagogy, and acknowledge that a professor still tunes count, emphasis, and interpretive frame per doctrine, so the quota is a floor the prompt guarantees, not the exam-design judgment itself.

### Persona 3: AI-in-Education Researcher
- [P1] Section VI.D paragraphs 7-8 ("only the adversarial Verify pass returned the actual language" and "The Verify prompt implements adversarial reading at the prompt level rather than as an external review process") — the section asserts that the three-persona adversarial architecture is effective on the strength of a single anecdote (n=1, the Prize Cases catch) and engages none of the contested NLP/learning-sciences literature on whether LLM self-critique and multi-agent debate actually improve accuracy (mixed results absent external feedback). This is an efficacy claim made without methodology. Distinct from PI-040, which is a citation-density gap about anchoring the design framings on more than the single Qian survey; this finding is that the architecture's efficacy is inferred from one anecdote. (PI-139)
  Suggested fix: in the next Polish/Cite tick on Section 06, reframe the Prize Cases passage as one illustrative instance rather than evidence of systematic benefit ("one documented instance in which the adversarial pass caught what five prior passes missed; the article does not claim a measured catch-rate"), add a one-clause acknowledgment that the LLM self-critique / multi-agent-debate efficacy literature is mixed, and add a forward pointer to Section XI's evaluation discussion; Du et al. 2023 multiagent-debate (already named in PI-040) is the natural cite.

- [P1] Section VII.C paragraphs 2-4 (the *Prize Cases* misquotation catch and the *Youngstown* "joined"-versus-concurred catch) — the section presents two textual-accuracy corrections as the substance of "Iterative Improvement Under Professorial Control," but catching a corrupted quotation and a vote-characterization slip is fact-checking and copy-editing, not the exercise or improvement of teaching judgment; neither story involves a professor making a pedagogical decision (both are automated persona catches). ### Persona 3: AI-in-Education Researcher

This is an efficacy claim without methodology, and the section engages none of the specific NLP/learning-sciences finding that LLM self-critique and multi-agent debate yield mixed accuracy gains absent external feedback. Distinct from PI-048 (the broad section-wide single-citation/literature-anchoring gap) and from PI-045 (the lessons-file improvement sentence); this finding is the narrow methodological one that two anecdotes cannot establish the architecture's efficacy. ### Persona 2: Legal Pedagogy Traditionalist
- [P1] Section VIII.E (paragraph 5) — the load-bearing label "a reviewed static site" and the sentence "The published page is a stable HTML and JSON artifact, written and verified by the rotation phases at publish time" let "reviewed" and "verified" be performed by the automated rotation (the Verify phase and its personas), yet a senior teacher reading "reviewed static site" will assume a human professor reviewed each published page; Section VIII never establishes per-page professorial sign-off, so "reviewed" silently upgrades automated verification into the professorial endorsement the phrase connotes. A twenty-five-year teacher reads "gating actor" and "reviewing and approving each layer" as the editor function, eliding the Socratic dialogue, the in-class hypothetical, and the live student confusion that are the professor's pedagogical work; the generalization claim then travels with teaching reduced to sign-off. ### Persona 1: Skeptical JLE Editor
- [P1] Section XI.A (paragraph 1) -- self-narrating meta-rhetoric: "The thesis is a method contribution, and the section states the disclaimer at the opening as the load-bearing claim the rest of the section builds on." The sentence describes the section's own rhetorical architecture to the reader instead of advancing the argument, the register a JLE editor reads as the author narrating the article's structure rather than writing it. ### Persona 2: Legal Pedagogy Traditionalist
- [P1] Section XI (the section as a whole, esp. XI.B paragraph 2 and XI.D paragraph 4) -- no comparator or baseline: the four-instrument plan measures use patterns and self-report on the static artifact (page views, navigation, time on page, perceived usefulness, think-aloud findability) but proposes no comparison against the casebook-plus-Socratic instruction the method supplements, so "what would count as success" cannot speak to whether the system helps relative to the established teaching tradition. A twenty-five-year teacher reads a success plan with no baseline as one that measures whether the artifact gets used, not whether it teaches better than what came before. Distinct from PI-104 (pedagogy-scope-silence, which is about the choice of metrics, not the absence of a comparison condition). (PI-158)
  Suggested fix: in the next Polish tick on Section 11, add a sentence either proposing a light comparator element (e.g., perceived usefulness relative to the casebook and class notes the same students use) or explicitly disclaiming comparative inference as out of scope so the absence is a stated boundary rather than a silent gap.

### Persona 3: AI-in-Education Researcher
- [P1] Section XI.B (paragraph 2) -- methodology named without rigor: the four instruments (a five-item Likert survey, Netlify analytics, three-to-five thirty-minute think-aloud sessions, and the disciplinary framing) are specified with no validity or reliability treatment for the Likert scale, no sampling frame or saturation logic for the think-aloud sessions, and no analysis plan, while XI.C (paragraph 3) invokes Bond et al.'s call for increased methodological rigor as the field-level frame the article "anticipates." A learning-sciences reviewer for Bond's own venue reads the section as claiming alignment with a rigor standard its instruments do not operationalize. Distinct from PI-104 (scope of what is measured) and PI-109 (Likert item-count specification). (PI-159)

### Persona 1: Skeptical JLE Editor
- [P1] Section XIV.B (paragraph 2, final sentence) -- self-narrating meta-rhetoric surfaced in prose: "The article earns a reader's trust precisely by bounding what it will and will not assert." The sentence narrates the rhetorical effect of the preceding disclaimers (it tells the reader the bounding earns trust) instead of letting the disclaimers do the work; a JLE editor reads it as the article praising its own scholarly restraint rather than exercising it. Fresh-site recurrence of the meta-rhetoric / voice-slip family (PI-022/PI-028/PI-036/PI-060/PI-141/PI-145/PI-153/PI-157/PI-161/PI-165). Distinct from PI-098 (replicability overclaim at the same section), which flags an overclaim rather than a self-narration. (PI-169)
  Suggested fix: in the next Polish tick on Section 14, delete "The article earns a reader's trust precisely by bounding what it will and will not assert" or recast it as a plain transition (e.g., "The article is as careful about what it declined to claim as about what it showed." already opens the paragraph), so the disclaimers stand without the prose announcing their effect.

### Persona 2: Legal Pedagogy Traditionalist
- [P1] Section XIV.C (paragraph 3) -- the conclusion enumerates the adopted contribution as "the typed schema, the verified-source discipline, the professorial gating, and the iterative loop," a list of authoring and curatorial competencies, and frames mastery of these as the faculty contribution, never distinguishing the labor of building and stewarding the artifact from the labor of teaching with it in a live classroom (Socratic dialogue, in-class hypotheticals, live student confusion). A twenty-five-year teacher reads a conclusion that equates skilled artifact-authorship with skilled teaching. On-brief ("confuses being a good editor with being a good teacher"); fresh Section 14 site of the editor-vs-teacher conflation family (PI-166 at Section 13). Distinct from PI-099 (which flags the conclusion implying the system substitutes for the live classroom); this flags the conclusion equating the editorial/curatorial method with teaching itself. (PI-170)
  Suggested fix: in the next Polish tick on Section 14, add a clause to XIV.C stating that the adopted method (schema, verified-source discipline, gating, loop) equips the artifact that supports the professor's live teaching rather than constituting that teaching, so the conclusion of a pedagogy article does not close by reducing the faculty role to authorship and gating.

### Persona 3: AI-in-Education Researcher

A learning-sciences reviewer reads the conclusion's architectural disclaimer as eliding what the static design gives up. On-brief ("engage with the chatbot-tutor literature as an alternative architecture and explain the architectural choice"); fresh Section 14 site, distinct from PI-100 (equity/accessibility at this section) and PI-167 (the Section 13 institutional-comparator instance). (PI-171)
  Suggested fix: in the next Polish or Draft tick on Section 14, add a clause to XIV.B noting the static architecture trades the adaptivity and interactive feedback the chatbot-tutor literature documents for build-time auditability and professorial review, cross-referencing Section XII's architectural contrast, so the conclusion frames the choice as a deliberate tradeoff rather than a one-way risk reduction.

### Persona 2: Legal Pedagogy Traditionalist
- [P1] Section VII (and cross-cutting through I.B, X.C, XII.B, XIII.C) — the spine claim that professorial judgment stayed in the loop ("The system did not replace professorial judgment; it made that judgment reusable, inspectable, and publishable") and the repeated assertion that the professor reviews and gates every layer (I.B note 2, VII.A, VII.E, X.C, XIII.C) are asserted throughout but never evidenced. The described system is an automated scheduled-task rotation firing on a cadence (VII.B; "every thirty minutes" per IX.C), no section shows where the professor actually inspected, how often, or what the professor changed, and XII.B concedes "the article does not measure that reviewer's catch rate." - [P1] Section XII.B (paragraphs 1–2) and footnote [^156] — the four-axis architectural contrast (review timing, error surface, update mechanism, accountability) treats "the chatbot-tutor alternative" as a representative category but anchors the whole category on a single platform paper, Sajja et al. 2023 ([^156]). ### Persona 2: Legal Pedagogy Traditionalist
- [P1] Section I.A (paragraph 1, the sentence "It seeks only to preserve its residue, the structure and reasoning that would otherwise leave with the class.") — the re-drafted introduction concedes that "the live performance is the irreplaceable act of teaching" and that the method "does not seek to replace it," which is the right posture, but it then defines the recoverable "residue" as "the structure and reasoning," quietly implying that the valuable part of teaching is what the system captures. To a twenty-five-year doctrinal teacher the part that matters most, the live Socratic exchange, the in-class hypothetical that adapts to a student's confusion, and the moment-by-moment reading of the room, is precisely *not* "structure and reasoning that can be preserved"; it is the performance the prior sentence calls irreplaceable. The article makes this concession well at XII.B ("a student who absorbs that map without contesting it forgoes the live Socratic contest"), but the introduction, where the residue claim is first planted, never signals it. This is the "confuses being a good editor with being a good teacher" risk PERSONAS.md names, surfacing at the article's most-read paragraph. Distinct from PI-029 (Section III cross-reference) and the actor-erasure PIs. (PI-184)

### Persona 7: Adversarial Reviewer ("Reviewer 2") [SUPPLEMENTAL — rotating in this tick per PERSONAS.md run_count-mod-3]
- [P1] Section X.E ("From a method to a second instance") in `manuscript/polished/section-10-generalization.md` lines 44-46 and the matched assembled-draft site at `manuscript/full-draft.md` lines 304-306, sentences "The strongest test of a method is whether a second person can run it, and this article reports one instance rather than two. The generalization claim is therefore an invitation as much as a result." — opens PI-219, a new finding on the article's strongest anticipated reviewer-2 attack the X.E paragraph does not currently close. The Pedagogy-Traditionalist-style hostile reviewer reads X.E and writes the rejection sentence: "The article asserts the method generalizes but reports one instance and offers no external comparable example. The generalizability of a method is earned through replication, comparable independent demonstrations, or a theoretical argument that the method's components have been independently validated; this paper delivers none of the three, only an invitation. n=1 case studies are not contributions to legal-education scholarship without one of those three legs, and this paper presents none." The article's existing defenses against the reduction — X.A's "method is the contribution" and X.E's "invitation as much as a result" — are labels rather than load-bearing rebuttals; they assert the method's transferability without naming a single external comparable digital-garden / knowledge-system / open-doctrinal-course effort that demonstrates the components have been independently exercised on a different doctrinal corpus. A hostile reviewer would write that the article needs to anticipate the "this is just a fancy outline / website" reduction explicitly and rebut it with something the article currently does not deliver: either a citation to a comparable replicated method in legal or higher education (the appropriate locus is X.D's case-doctrine assumption discussion, X.E's second-instance specification, or XII.E's risks discussion), or a much sharper specification of what an n=2 attempt would test against, what would falsify the method's transferability, and what minimum-viable-instance-2 design would license a generalization claim that the article currently asserts. The standing-but-unaddressed PI-088 (X.A-X.B literature-gap, Cite-routed) and PI-J​LE-LEGAL-ED-LIT family already capture the adjacent literature-anchor gap, but PI-219 is distinct from those at the rhetorical-anticipation axis: the section needs a sentence or two that name the strongest counterargument before the article's own defense lands, on the standard "anticipate then rebut" reviewer-anticipation pattern that hostile reviewers explicitly look for and that the X.A-through-X.E arc currently glides past.
  Suggested fix: in the next Polish or Cite tick on Section 10, add one substantive paragraph to X.E (between the existing "The strongest test..." and "The corpus..." sentences) that names the n=1 critique explicitly and rebuts it on three named legs: (a) cite a comparable course-knowledge-system or open-doctrinal-course effort in legal or higher education at the footnote level (candidate anchors per the Section X bibliography in Article-Workplan.md include Carnegie-tradition digital-courseware efforts or the open-casebook landscape Section XIII handles); (b) commit to a falsifiable minimum-viable-instance-2 specification — e.g., "a procedure or torts course built on the same three-page-type schema with the same six-phase rotation and the same professorial-checkpoint discipline, reporting at the same depth as this article on which schema fields needed retyping and which carried, would constitute a falsification test"; and (c) acknowledge the reduction directly in X.A or X.E with one sentence on the order of "A skeptical reader will ask whether a single course built with named tools is a method or merely a build; the method claim depends on a second instance reporting whether the components carried." The three legs together convert X.E's "invitation" framing from an unrebutted reviewer-attack surface into an explicit anticipate-and-rebut pattern. PI-219 status: open (new). Routes Section 10 polish_status: needs_polish (already set; no status delta). New PI opened: PI-219 type reviewer2-anticipation-gap.

### Persona 2: Legal Pedagogy Traditionalist
- [P1] Section X.E ("From a method to a second instance"), the run-268-inserted PI-219 leg b sentence "The bounded answer specifies a minimum viable second instance: a comparable doctrinal course built and reviewed by a different faculty member under the same checkpoints." in `manuscript/polished/section-10-generalization.md` line 44 and `manuscript/full-draft.md` line 312 — opens PI-220, a new finding on the definition tension between X.E's minimum-viable-instance specification and X.D's own concession that "[a] rules-and-procedure course such as Civil Procedure, or a heavily statutory course such as Tax, fits that assumption less cleanly, because the load-bearing unit is a rule or a code section rather than a leading case." X.D acknowledges that the case-method assumption breaks for procedure and statutory courses and that "the three-folder taxonomy would need re-typing before it could carry the work" — precisely the harder extension a Civil Procedure professor would be asked to test. X.E then specifies the minimum viable second instance as "a comparable doctrinal course" without defining what makes a course "comparable" for the n=2 purpose. A Civil Procedure professor reading X.D and X.E in sequence reads the article as saying simultaneously "Civ Pro fits less cleanly" (X.D, in tension) and "a comparable doctrinal course is what would test the method" (X.E, narrowed). The strongest falsification test, in the Pedagogy Traditionalist's reading, would be precisely an n=2 attempt on a non-case-method course (because that is where X.D concedes the method's purchase is weakest), but X.E's "comparable" qualifier elides that very test by implicit case-method scoping. The X.D / X.E pair currently advances two incompatible n=2 framings: the one that tests the method's hardest edge (X.D's concession) and the one that tests the method's easiest extension (X.E's "comparable").
  Suggested fix: in the next Polish tick on Section 10, either narrow X.E's "a comparable doctrinal course" to "a comparable case-method doctrinal course" (making the case-method scope explicit and acknowledging that the X.D rules-and-procedure / statutory extension is a SEPARATE n=2 question), or broaden X.E to specify two falsification tracks — an easier track ("a comparable case-method doctrinal course such as Torts or Contracts") and a harder track ("a rules-and-procedure or statutory course, where X.D acknowledges the three-folder taxonomy would need re-typing before it could carry the work"). The two-track framing reads more honestly against X.D's own concession and gives a Civ Pro professor a place to stand inside the n=2 invitation rather than reading themselves as excluded by the "comparable" qualifier. Optional pair: add one footnote to X.E cross-referencing X.D ("[^N] *See infra* Section X.D (acknowledging the harder extension)") so a reader sees the two-track structure without having to hold X.D and X.E in mind simultaneously. PI-220 status: open (new). Routes Section 10 polish_status: ready_for_stitch -> needs_polish (regress; the run-268 Polish closed PI-154 and PI-219 legs b+c but introduced the X.E / X.D tension this finding captures, a same-tick downstream consequence the run-268 Polish narrative did not anticipate). New PI opened: PI-220 type two-track-n2-framing-gap.

### Persona 5: Practitioner-Adopter (Reproducibility) [SUPPLEMENTAL — rotating in this tick per PERSONAS.md run_count-mod-3]
- [P1] Section X.E ("From a method to a second instance"), the run-268-inserted PI-219 leg b sentence "The bounded answer specifies a minimum viable second instance: a comparable doctrinal course built and reviewed by a different faculty member under the same checkpoints." in `manuscript/polished/section-10-generalization.md` line 44 and `manuscript/full-draft.md` line 312 — opens PI-221, a new finding on the spec-versus-narrate gap in the new minimum-viable-instance specification. The sentence specifies "the same checkpoints" without naming what the checkpoints are, where they live, or how an adopter would operationalize "the same" against a different course. A mid-career law professor at a teaching-focused school, reading X.E as the n=2 invitation the article extends, has no way from the X.E text alone to know whether "the same checkpoints" means the six-phase rotation, the per-phase professorial gating actor, the adversarial review pass, the LESSONS-file memory between runs, all four, or something else; Section VII (Iterative Improvement Under Professorial Control) defines all of these, but X.E does not cross-reference Section VII. The Persona 5 reading frame: the adopter who wants to BUILD the same thing (not admire it) hits the X.E specification, asks "what counts as the same checkpoints?", and has nowhere to look without re-reading Section VII end-to-end and inferring which structural feature is checkpoint-load-bearing. This is the canonical Persona 5 defect class: "the method is NARRATED (what the author did) rather than SPECIFIED (what a reader must do to replicate it): missing steps, tools, decisions, or thresholds an adopter would need." The X.E spec gestures at a falsifiability standard ("a comparable doctrinal course built and reviewed under the same checkpoints") but does not give the adopter the operational threshold the standard requires.
  Suggested fix: in the next Polish tick on Section 10, add a cross-reference clause to the X.E PI-219 leg b sentence pointing the adopter to Section VII as the checkpoints definition site; for example, append "[^N] *See supra* Section VII (defining the per-phase professorial checkpoints the second-instance specification refers to)" to the end of the leg b sentence, so an adopter reading X.E in isolation can trace "the same checkpoints" to the source definition. Alternatively, expand the leg b sentence to name the load-bearing checkpoint components inline: "a comparable doctrinal course built and reviewed by a different faculty member under the same per-phase professorial gating, the same forced-finding adversarial review pass, and the same LESSONS-file memory between runs." The inline-naming option carries the spec without requiring the adopter to chase the cross-reference, which is the Persona 5 reproducibility preference; the cross-reference option is lighter on word budget. Either fix converts X.E from a falsifiability-standard-without-an-operational-threshold into a falsifiability-standard-with-a-named-threshold. PI-221 status: open (new). Routes Section 10 polish_status: needs_polish (already routed by PI-220 this tick; no status delta). New PI opened: PI-221 type adopter-checkpoint-specification-gap.

### Persona 1: Skeptical JLE Editor
- [P1] Section IX.A, paragraph 1 sentence 2 "The existing record’s single anchored figure, about sixty percent of weekly Claude usage consumed to reach the 198-page progress snapshot, does not split by phase, by labor category, or by dollar count.[^114] [^115]" in `manuscript/full-draft.md` line 276 and the matched site at `manuscript/polished/section-09-cost-and-labor.md` paragraph 1 of IX.A (post-frontmatter line corresponding to the post-Polish opener) — opens PI-222, a new finding on a residue-of-eviction attribution gap surfaced by the run-273 L-057 sweep that the run-273 Polish narrative explicitly endorsed without naming. The run-273 Polish rewrote the IX.A opener from "The workplan names a cost-and-labor table..." to "An honest cost-and-labor table is essential for a method paper of this kind: without one, the cost arguments throughout Sections III, VIII, and IX read as unfalsifiable", which cleanly removes the workplan attribution and reads honestly. But the next sentence — "The existing record’s single anchored figure, about sixty percent of weekly Claude usage..." — still names "the existing record" as the source of the 60% Claude-usage figure without disclosing whose existing record or what artifact-level the existing record is anchored to. A JLE editor reading IX.A for the first time, with no access to the L-057 worked example or the run-273 narrative, asks "the existing record of what?" and has nowhere to look in the body to resolve the referent; the [^114] App. C forward-reference resolves the figure itself but does not anchor the phrase "the existing record" in the body prose. Pre-eviction, the workplan-attribution clause had carried the anchor implicitly ("the workplan names the cost-and-labor table as one of the five most-important gaps, and identifies the existing record’s single anchored figure as a hand-wave"); post-eviction, the "existing record" phrase floats unanchored. This is the residue-of-eviction defect class L-057 / L-058 do not yet name explicitly (the lessons name the eviction-as-prohibition rule but do not yet name the eviction-blast-radius rule that an L-057 / L-058 sweep can leave anchored-by-attribution phrases stranded in body). The defect is small but real and runs precisely against the section's IX.A unfalsifiability-thesis opener: a reader who cannot anchor "the existing record" cannot independently evaluate whether the 60% figure is even claimed to be the right anchor.
  Suggested fix: in the next Polish tick on Section 09, rewrite IX.A sentence 2 to anchor "the existing record" to the article's own cost-log or Appendix C scaffold. Two equivalent forms work: (a) "The article's existing cost record, the rolling cost log committed at each scheduled-task tick and reconstructed in Appendix C, carries a single anchored figure -- about sixty percent of weekly Claude usage consumed to reach the 198-page progress snapshot -- and that figure does not split by phase, by labor category, or by dollar count.[^114] [^115]" (29 words for the lead clause, anchoring the existing record to the rolling cost log via App. C); (b) "Section IX inherits one anchored figure from the cost-and-labor record this article has kept against itself: about sixty percent of weekly Claude usage consumed to reach the 198-page progress snapshot. That figure does not split by phase, by labor category, or by dollar count.[^114] [^115]" (29 + 16 = 45 words, anchoring the existing record to the article's own self-reflexive recordkeeping). Either form replaces the unanchored "the existing record" referent with an article-internal self-anchor that requires no external prior knowledge to evaluate. The fix is single-sentence Polish work that the next Polish tick on Section 09 can execute alongside the PI-222 anchor work. PI-222 status: open (new). Routes Section 09 polish_status: ready_for_stitch -> needs_polish (already routed by the Persona 4 Provenance audit return this tick on the 4.2 < 4.5 score; no double-routing). New PI opened: PI-222 type residue-of-eviction-attribution-gap.

### Persona 7: Adversarial Reviewer ("Reviewer 2") [SUPPLEMENTAL — rotating in this tick per PERSONAS.md run_count-mod-3]
- [P1] Section IX.A paragraph 1 sentence 1 "An honest cost-and-labor table is essential for a method paper of this kind: without one, the cost arguments throughout Sections III, VIII, and IX read as unfalsifiable." in `manuscript/full-draft.md` line 276 and `manuscript/polished/section-09-cost-and-labor.md` IX.A line 1 paired with the IX.E closing sentence "Three items are still outstanding within Section IX: the master cost-and-labor table reconstruction itself remains owed,[^128] the external pricing lookups for Anthropic API pricing, Netlify free-tier and upgrade pricing, and any metered MCP service prices are deferred to a later citation pass, and a person-hours backfill across the four labor categories with honest confidence ranges remains owed, the methodology footnoted at the row level so a reader can replicate against the same vault and git history.[^129]" in `manuscript/full-draft.md` line 292 — opens PI-223, a new finding on a structural anticipate-and-rebut gap that the run-273 L-057 sweep unintentionally surfaced by replacing the workplan-attribution opener with an unfalsifiability-admission opener. The defect class: the article concedes its own cost arguments are currently unfalsifiable absent a master cost-and-labor table (IX.A), then the section closes (IX.E) by acknowledging the master table "remains owed". A hostile peer reviewer reading IX.A and IX.E in sequence writes the rejection sentence as: "The article concedes at IX.A that without an honest cost-and-labor table its cost arguments throughout Sections III, VIII, and IX read as unfalsifiable, and acknowledges at IX.E that the master cost-and-labor table reconstruction itself remains owed. This is a self-confessed methodological failure: the article should either deliver at least one column of the table or remove the cost arguments that depend on it. The submission package as it stands contains an explicit admission that one of its three load-bearing argument families lacks the verification artifact the article itself names as required, and the JLE editorial board cannot accept a method paper whose own author admits the method's cost arguments are unfalsifiable at submission time." This is exactly the kind of Reviewer-2 fatal finding the supplemental Persona 7 is rotated in to surface before a real reviewer does. The article's gap-flagging discipline at IX.E saves the finding from a P0 (the article does explicitly flag the gap rather than hiding it; the unsupported_claims-with-reason discipline applies at the section-level), but the structural defect is real: an article-level unfalsifiability-admission is heavier than a section-level gap-flag, and IX.A's opener writes the admission at the article-level scope ("throughout Sections III, VIII, and IX read as unfalsifiable") not the section-level scope. Removing the AI angle from the cost arguments would still leave the article with the published cost-log methodology and the per-tick build-narrative discipline at IX.C / IX.D, which is more than a generic digital-garden post offers — but the cost-arguments-without-the-table problem is independent of the AI angle. The single strongest counterargument the article failed to anticipate at this finding's site is the "deliver one column or remove the claim" counterargument: the L-057 sweep cleared the workplan-attribution but did not pair the cleared opener with a bounded scope, so the unfalsifiability-admission reads as absolute when the section's other subsections (IX.B hosting-cost two-state, IX.C wall-clock anchors, IX.D tiered-loading) do carry their own verifiable data.
  Suggested fix: in the next Polish tick on Section 09, do ONE of the following two things (preferably both): (a) Bound the IX.A unfalsifiability-admission scope. Rewrite IX.A sentence 1 to scope the admission to the per-phase / per-category / per-dollar breakdown that the master table would carry, not to the section's whole cost-and-labor scope. For example: "An honest cost-and-labor table is essential for the per-phase, per-category, and per-dollar breakdown a method paper of this kind owes its readers; the sections that follow carry the cost claims this article can already support at the architectural and wall-clock level, and the master table the next subsection enumerates is what closes the per-phase, per-category, per-dollar gap." (53 words for the lead sentence pair, replacing the current 28-word opener with a bounded-scope version). The fix preserves the methodological-stakes signaling that L-057 was after but limits the unfalsifiability-admission to the per-table scope rather than the whole-section scope, so the reviewer cannot read "the cost arguments throughout Sections III, VIII, and IX read as unfalsifiable" as a section-wide admission. (b) Deliver at least one column of the master table inline. Add a short table or footnote-anchored enumeration at IX.E that delivers the master table's hosting-cost column (which IX.B already has the data for: zero marginal on free tier under normal operation, blocked when credits exhaust; the three May 7, 2026 timestamps; the Netlify CLI vs MCP fallback split) and the wall-clock column (which IX.C already has the data for: fast-deploy ~6sec, clean CLI ~16s build + ~8s upload, verification budget ~10-15s, vault every 30 min, article every 2 hours). The two-column-delivered version turns the "table remains owed" closer into "two of the five columns are delivered inline; the per-phase token-spend column, the per-category person-hours column, and the per-dollar API-pricing column are deferred to a later citation pass". The two-column delivery converts the section's structural anticipate-and-rebut gap into a bounded gap that names what is delivered and what is deferred. Either fix retires PI-223; both together retire PI-223 and lift criterion 1 claim_coverage on the Section 09 audit by adding mapped claims for the delivered columns. PI-223 status: open (new). Routes Section 09 polish_status: ready_for_stitch -> needs_polish (already routed by the Persona 4 Provenance audit return this tick on the 4.2 < 4.5 score and by PI-222; no quadruple-routing). ### Persona 1: Skeptical JLE Editor
- [P1] Section XIII.B paragraph closing sentence "A course knowledge system is therefore best read as faculty scholarship of teaching coupled with institutional stewardship, not as an unfunded individual burden." in `manuscript/full-draft.md` line 381 and the matched site at `manuscript/polished/section-13-institutional-implications.md` XIII.B paragraph closing sentence — opens PI-224, a new finding on a scholarship-of-teaching label asserted as a settled framing without a legal-education-canon anchor. The XIII.B closer labels the work as "faculty scholarship of teaching" — a contested term-of-art in legal education with a substantial scholarly literature behind it (Boyer's *Scholarship Reconsidered* (1990) on the four scholarships including scholarship of teaching; Sturm & Guinier on teaching as scholarship; ABA Standard 405 on scholarship recognition; the SoTL-in-law literature this article's L-062 mandate names as the venue-fit anchor). The XIII.B closer asserts the framing with no citation to any of that literature; the adjacent XIII.C paragraph then explicitly admits the framing is "contested" ("Whether a versioned, citable, publicly served course artifact counts as scholarship is itself contested"). A JLE editor reading XIII.B and XIII.C in sequence sees the article asserting at the end of XIII.B precisely what XIII.C immediately concedes is contested, and asks: by whose authority is the work "best read" as scholarship of teaching when the article admits one paragraph later that the recognition question is unresolved? This is a load-bearing legal-education-canon-anchor gap on the kind of claim a JLE reviewer is most likely to challenge (the article reads as legal-education scholarship per L-062 mandate, and the scholarship-of-teaching framing is a legal-education-canon claim that must be anchored to a legal-education-canon source). The defect is the inverse of the L-057/L-058 eviction-class: not an internal-artifact-leak but a missing-external-canon-anchor on a claim the editor expects to be canon-anchored.
  Suggested fix: in the next Polish or Cite tick on Section 13, anchor the XIII.B "best read as faculty scholarship of teaching" framing to one or two legal-education-canon citations. Two equivalent forms work: (a) Rewrite XIII.B closer to soften the assertion: "A course knowledge system can be read as faculty scholarship of teaching coupled with institutional stewardship rather than as an unfunded individual burden, though Section XIII.C develops the recognition question that framing leaves open." (45 words; softens "best read" to "can be read", adds the XIII.C cross-reference). (b) Add one footnote anchoring the scholarship-of-teaching framing to Boyer's *Scholarship Reconsidered* (1990) and/or to Sturm and Guinier's account of teaching-as-scholarship in legal education (specific cite to be confirmed against `research/legal-ed-scholarship.md` at the next Cite tick). Either form retires PI-224 by anchoring the contested framing the XIII.C "contested" admission requires. PI-224 status: open (new). Routes Section 13 polish_status: ready_for_stitch → needs_polish (already routed by the Persona 4 Provenance audit return this tick on the 4.0 < 4.5 score; no double-routing). New PI opened: PI-224 type scholarship-of-teaching-label-without-legal-ed-canon-anchor.

### Persona 2: Legal Pedagogy Traditionalist
- [P1] Section XIII.C paragraph "The recognition question follows. Whether a versioned, citable, publicly served course artifact counts as scholarship is itself contested, and the institution must decide whether to treat it as teaching infrastructure or as scholarship for review and promotion. That decision stays open, but the inspectable, versioned artifact makes either form of recognition assessable: a reviewer can read what was published and trace who reviewed it." in `manuscript/full-draft.md` line 385 and `manuscript/polished/section-13-institutional-implications.md` XIII.C closing paragraph — re-confirms PI-166 (editor-vs-teacher-conflation, section 13) in a narrowed form. The run-236 Polish narrative recorded PI-166 closure work via a XIII.B sentence "teaching with the artifact remains the professor's classroom craft" added to distinguish artifact-building labor from in-class teaching; PI-166 was kept OPEN for "next Verify to certify the concessions satisfy the persona concerns." This Verify pass certifies the XIII.B leg as adequately closing the conflation at the artifact-vs-teaching axis but finds the XIII.C recognition paragraph immediately reopens the conflation at a different axis: XIII.C frames recognition in purely scholarship-publishing terms (versioned, citable, publicly served; review and promotion; the inspectable artifact as the assessable object) without re-anchoring those terms to the live in-class teaching the XIII.B sentence preserved. A Civ Pro traditionalist reading XIII.C after the XIII.B distinguishing sentence reads the article as silently retreating from the XIII.B craft-of-teaching preservation: the recognition question is asked entirely about the artifact's scholarly-output status, never about whether the in-class teaching the artifact serves remains the locus of pedagogy. The traditionalist's concern is not that the artifact shouldn't count as scholarship — that is genuinely contested and the article is right to flag it — but that the article's recognition discussion treats the artifact as the assessable thing and silently elides whether the live teaching (the Socratic dialogue, the in-class hypothetical, the live student confusion) remains primary. This is the editor-vs-teacher conflation re-emerging at the recognition-framing axis after the XIII.B closure addressed it only at the artifact-building-labor axis.
  Suggested fix: in the next Polish tick on Section 13, add one sentence to XIII.C's closing paragraph re-anchoring the recognition discussion to the live teaching the artifact serves. For example, after "a reviewer can read what was published and trace who reviewed it", append: "What the artifact does not capture, and what the recognition discussion should not silently elide, is the live in-class teaching the artifact serves: the Socratic dialogue, the in-class hypothetical, and the live student confusion that the casebook tradition makes its locus of pedagogy and that this method preserves rather than replaces." (47 words; re-anchors recognition to live teaching and the casebook tradition's locus). The addition does not commit to a position on whether the artifact should count as scholarship — it preserves XIII.C's "stays open" framing — but it closes the conflation at the recognition-framing axis where the XIII.B addition could not reach. PI-166 status: open → open (re-confirmed at narrowed scope; XIII.B leg satisfied per run-236 Polish concession, XIII.C leg still open). Routes Section 13 polish_status:ready_for_stitch → needs_polish (already routed by the Persona 4 Provenance audit return this tick; no double-routing). No new PI opened.

### Persona 3: AI-in-Education Researcher
- [P1] Section XIII.D paragraph "The institutional implication is a modest, replicable infrastructure investment offered as an invitation rather than a mandate, subject to a capacity gap in library, instructional-design, and information-technology stewardship that Section XII develops.[^171]" in `manuscript/full-draft.md` line 389 and `manuscript/polished/section-13-institutional-implications.md` XIII.D — re-confirms PI-095 (equity-and-accessibility-gap, section 13) in a narrowed institution-side-closed / student-side-open form. The run-236 Polish narrative recorded PI-095 closure work via the XIII.D capacity-gap clause added with a new internal [^8] (assembled [^171]) supra Section XII forward-reference; PI-095 was kept OPEN for "next Verify to certify the concessions satisfy the persona concerns." This Verify pass certifies the institution-side capacity-gap leg as adequately closing the equity concern on the institutional axis (the run-236 clause names library, instructional-design, and information-technology stewardship as unevenly distributed across institutions, and forwards to Section XII for the fuller treatment) but finds the section is silent on the STUDENT-SIDE accessibility axis. A learning-sciences reviewer reading XIII.D in isolation reads the equity discussion as institution-facing only: the article handles the question of whether under-resourced schools can adopt the method but says nothing about whether students at those schools (or any schools) can use the deployed artifact equitably. Section XII.E does carry the student-side accessibility surface (broadband presupposition, screen-reader behavior of hash-route navigation, 300-dpi PDFs assuming render-capable devices, Canvas enrollment gate, analytics privacy stance) per `manuscript/full-draft.md` line 369 — but XIII.D's "subject to a capacity gap... that Section XII develops" forward-reference points the reader at Section XII's institution-side capacity treatment, not at XII.E's student-side accessibility treatment, and a reader who follows the [^171] pointer arrives at the wrong leg of Section XII. The chatbot-tutor architectural-alternative literature this Persona was rotated in to read (the Bond meta-review's equity prong; Magesh's access-cost-and-hallucination concerns; the field-level frame at XII.E) is consistent with the institution-side concession but does not extend to the student-side leg the section as published omits.
  Suggested fix: in the next Polish tick on Section 13, do ONE of the following: (a) Expand the XIII.D capacity-gap clause to name both legs of the access constraint. For example, rewrite XIII.D's closing sentence to: "The institutional implication is a modest, replicable infrastructure investment offered as an invitation rather than a mandate, subject to a capacity gap in library, instructional-design, and information-technology stewardship and to a use-side accessibility surface (broadband, screen-reader behavior, render-capable devices, enrollment gating) that Section XII develops." (50 words; adds the four-item student-side accessibility leg with parenthetical enumeration). (b) Add one additional footnote at XIII.D forwarding specifically to XII.E rather than to Section XII as a whole, so a reader arrives at the student-side accessibility treatment rather than the institution-side capacity treatment. The (a) form is preferred because the institutional-implications section should not depend on a forward-reference to surface a leg of its own equity discussion; the (b) form retires the routing-gap but leaves the body silent on student-side accessibility. PI-095 status: open → open (re-confirmed at narrowed scope; institution-side leg satisfied per run-236 Polish concession, student-side leg still open). Routes Section 13 polish_status:ready_for_stitch → needs_polish (already routed by the Persona 4 Provenance audit return this tick; no double-routing). No new PI opened.

### Persona 6: Structural / Developmental Editor [SUPPLEMENTAL — rotating in this tick per PERSONAS.md run_count-mod-3 = 280 mod 3 = 1]
- [P1] The Section XII → Section XIII seam at `manuscript/full-draft.md` lines 371-373 reading from XII.E closing sentence "Collaboration is the prong a single-author case study can least claim, which is one reason the article offers the method as its replicable contribution rather than the artifact alone." directly into the cold XIII.A opener "Legal education already supports open educational resources. The Harvard Law School Library Innovation Lab operates H2O Open Casebooks, the MIT Press and Harvard Law School Library jointly launched an open casebook series built on the H2O platform in 2022, and CALI's eLangdell Press distributes openly licensed casebooks and course materials." paired with the heavy concentration of supra Section XII forward-references inside Section 13 itself ([^165] at XIII.A, [^169] at XIII.C, [^171] at XIII.D — three of Section 13's nine footnotes all point readers backward to Section XII) — opens PI-225, a new finding on a structural seam-and-boundary defect. The defect class: Section XIII reads as Section XII's appendix because three of its nine footnotes ([^165], [^169], [^171]) are supra Section XII forward-references that defer the architectural-contrast, governance-traceability, and capacity-gap legs of the institutional-implications argument back to Section XII. A reader following any one of the three pointers leaves Section XIII to find the substantive claim's home in Section XII, then has to navigate back to Section XIII to recover the institutional-implications context. The cumulative effect across three forward-references is that Section XIII's argument hovers above Section XII's content without ever standing on its own. Worse, the XII.E → XIII.A seam has no bridging sentence: XII.E closes on Bond-collaboration as the limit of a single-author case study and then XIII.A opens cold with H2O / MIT / CALI as if the open-educational-resource landscape were a fresh topic, not the natural pivot from the just-closed risk-and-limits discussion. A reader reading the article in sequence reads the XII → XIII pivot as a topic change rather than as the article's planned move from "here are the risks and limits" to "here is what the institution should do about them given the existing open-resource landscape." The architecture-of-the-whole-manuscript view this Persona is rotated in to surface finds the section boundary blurry on three axes: the seam has no bridging sentence, the section depends on three supra Section XII forward-references for substantive content, and the section's claim of "institutional implications" reads as a restatement of Section XII's institution-side concerns rather than as a distinct argument about institutional choice and adoption. The defect is structural rather than line-level: Section XIII is doing real argumentative work (the open-resource landscape framing at XIII.A, the labor-and-roles distribution at XIII.B, the governance-and-recognition discussion at XIII.C, the adoption-invitation question at XIII.D), but the seam and the supra-XII-dependence make that work read as derivative of Section XII's content.
  Suggested fix: in the next Polish or Stitch tick on Section 13, do BOTH of the following: (a) Add one bridging sentence at the XII.E → XIII.A seam (either at the end of XII.E or at the start of XIII.A) that pivots explicitly from risk-and-limits to institutional-implications. For example, append at end of XII.E: "Those risks and limits set the institutional-implications agenda that follows: how the method fits the open-educational-resource landscape, how its labor distributes across faculty and institutional stewardship, how its governance is recognized, and what the institution must decide about adoption." (40 words; explicit forward-pivot naming the four XIII subsections). Or insert at start of XIII.A: "Against the risks and limits the prior section developed, the institutional implications begin with the existing open-educational-resource landscape." (19 words; explicit backward-pivot from Section XII). (b) Re-anchor at least one of the three supra Section XII forward-references ([^165], [^169], [^171]) to its natural in-article home: [^169] (the versioned-commit + named-reviewer operational claim) should forward-reference Section VII (gating) and Section VIII (versioning, deployment) rather than Section XII — the operational claim's natural home is the section that documents the gating-and-versioning architecture, not the section that develops the risks. [^171] (the capacity-gap clause) should be split: the institution-side capacity gap stays at Section XII, and a separate forward-reference to XII.E's student-side accessibility surface should be added per the Persona 3 PI-095 re-confirm this tick. The (b) re-anchoring reduces Section 13's supra-XII dependence from 3-of-9 footnotes to 1-of-9 (only [^165] at XIII.A would remain, where the architectural-contrast claim is genuinely a risk-and-limits cross-reference) and lets the section stand on its own. PI-225 status: open (new). Routes Section 13 polish_status: ready_for_stitch → needs_polish (already routed by the Persona 4 Provenance audit return this tick on the 4.0 < 4.5 score and by PI-224 and the PI-166 + PI-095 re-confirms; no quintuple-routing). New PI opened: PI-225 type structural-seam-and-supra-XII-dependence-defect.
