### Persona 1: Skeptical JLE Editor
- [P1] Section XIV.A paragraph 1 sentences "The opener was not automation. It was a method." in `manuscript/full-draft.md` line 397 and `manuscript/polished/section-14-conclusion.md` XIV.A paragraph 1, paired with XIV.C paragraph 1 sentences "The system did not replace professorial judgment; it made that judgment reusable, inspectable, and publishable." (line 405) and "The adopted method equips an artifact that supports the professor's live teaching rather than constituting that teaching." (line 405) — opens PI-226, a new finding on a recurring meta-rhetorical "X was not Y, it was Z" pattern appearing at three sites in 400 conclusion words. The pattern is the article's signature "negate-then-reframe" rhetorical move: the conclusion uses it to pivot from a negation ("was not automation," "did not replace professorial judgment," "rather than constituting that teaching") to an affirmative reframe in three consecutive paragraphs, with two of the three occurrences in XIV.C alone. A JLE editor reading the conclusion sees the rhetorical structure recurring at slogan density — three pivots in 400 words is roughly one every 130 words, well above the article's body-section register where the same pattern appears at lower frequency. The defect is voice slippage at the conclusion's load-bearing site: the article's recurring sentence ("The system did not replace professorial judgment; it made that judgment reusable, inspectable, and publishable.") is the third canonical placement per L-002 and is the load-bearing slogan the article earns by using it once per body site; but XIV.A's "The opener was not automation. It was a method." precedes it and XIV.C's "supports the professor's live teaching rather than constituting that teaching" follows it, sandwiching the canonical recurring sentence between two cousins of the same rhetorical move. A JLE editor reading the three-in-a-row negate-then-reframe sequence in the conclusion would read it as the article slogan-stacking rather than developing an argument, blurring the canonical recurring sentence's earned weight. Distinct from PI-098 (replicability-overclaim, the XIV.A "replicable process … can follow" assertion) and PI-186 (pedagogical-displacement-disclaimer-gap, the XIV.B candor-enumeration omission of the XII.B concession); this is a voice-and-register finding on the negate-then-reframe pattern density, not a substantive overclaim finding and not a missing disclaimer.
  Suggested fix: in the next Polish tick on Section 14, retire one of the two cousin instances bracketing the canonical recurring sentence. Two equivalent forms work: (a) Rewrite XIV.A's "The opener was not automation. It was a method." to drop the negate-then-reframe form and state the affirmative directly: "The opener was a method." (or merge into the surrounding sentence: "It closes by naming what opened that archive: not automation but a method, one in which generative AI, structured source materials..."). This drops the XIV.A cousin and preserves the canonical XIV.C recurring sentence as the conclusion's signature slogan. (b) Rewrite XIV.C's "The adopted method equips an artifact that supports the professor's live teaching rather than constituting that teaching." to drop the "rather than constituting" cousin and use the simpler affirmative form: "The adopted method equips an artifact that supports the professor's live teaching." This drops the XIV.C cousin downstream of the canonical recurring sentence and preserves XIV.A's framing. Either form lifts the density from one-per-130-words to one-per-200-words, restoring the canonical recurring sentence's distinctive weight. The (a) form is preferred because XIV.A's "was not automation" pivot is the more conspicuous slogan-form on first read (the binary against automation is the kind of phrase a JLE editor would flag as Silicon Valley register); (b) is the lighter touch. PI-226 status: open (new). Routes Section 14 polish_status: ready_for_stitch → needs_polish (already routed by the Persona 4 Provenance audit return this tick on the 4.4 < 4.5 score; no double-routing). New PI opened: PI-226 type voice-and-register-negate-then-reframe-pattern-density.

### Persona 2: Legal Pedagogy Traditionalist
- [P1] Section XIV.C paragraph 1 opening sentence "Across every layer, the expert never left the loop." in `manuscript/full-draft.md` line 405 and `manuscript/polished/section-14-conclusion.md` XIV.C paragraph 1 — re-confirms PI-099 (live-classroom-substitution, Section 14) in a narrowed live-teaching-named-but-loop-elides-classroom-judgment-locus form. The run-254 Polish narrative recorded PI-170 closure via the XIV.C "supports the professor's live teaching rather than constituting that teaching" insertion (run-280 frontmatter records PI-170 archived as resolved). This Verify pass certifies the XIV.C live-teaching-mention leg as adequately closing the silence at the artifact-vs-teaching axis (a traditionalist reading XIV.C now sees the article explicitly disavow that the artifact constitutes teaching), but finds the section's framing of "the loop" still elides the live-classroom locus of professorial judgment. A Civ Pro traditionalist reads "Across every layer, the expert never left the loop" and asks: what is the loop? Tracing the loop's referent backward through the section, XIV.C "the iterative loop" names it as one of the four method elements ("the typed schema, the verified-source discipline, the professorial gating, and the iterative loop"), and the [^181] *See supra* Section XIII pointer resolves the loop to the build-and-review pipeline the institutional-implications section describes — not to the live Socratic dialogue, in-class hypothetical, or live student-confusion exchange the traditionalist considers primary. The article's "loop" is the build-time review loop, not the in-class teaching loop. The conclusion's "expert never left the loop" claim is therefore true of the build-time pipeline and silent about the in-class teaching loop, where the most central form of professorial judgment lives. The XIV.C "supports … live teaching rather than constituting" insertion (PI-170 closure) makes the disavowal explicit but does not anchor "live teaching" to its canonical traditionalist particulars — Socratic dialogue, in-class hypotheticals, the live reading of student confusion that Section I.A names at line 51 — and does not say that the article's "loop" framing is a build-time loop distinct from the classroom loop. A traditionalist reading XIV.C in isolation would read "the expert never left the loop" as the article claiming the professor's classroom-judgment locus is captured by the pipeline, which is not the article's actual position (Section I.A explicitly names the live performance as irreplaceable, and the method does not seek to replace it). The narrowed leg is therefore: PI-099's "silent on the live classroom" complaint is partially closed by the XIV.C live-teaching mention, but the conclusion's "loop" framing still elides the classroom-judgment locus and the canonical particulars (Socratic dialogue / hypotheticals / live student confusion) that anchor the traditionalist's reading.
  Suggested fix: in the next Polish tick on Section 14, add one sentence to XIV.C distinguishing the article's "loop" from the in-class teaching loop, and naming the canonical traditionalist particulars. For example, after "the expert never left the loop" insert: "The loop here is the build-and-review pipeline that prepares the artifact, not the in-class teaching loop where the Socratic dialogue, the in-class hypothetical, and the live reading of student confusion remain irreplaceable per Section I.A." (38 words; explicit distinction between build-time and classroom loops, names the canonical traditionalist particulars, anchors them with the Section I.A cross-reference). Alternatively, rewrite the existing "supports the professor's live teaching rather than constituting that teaching" sentence to name the particulars inline: "The adopted method equips an artifact that supports the professor's live teaching — the Socratic dialogue, the in-class hypothetical, and the live reading of student confusion — rather than constituting that teaching." (28-word version, +14 words from current). The first form is preferred because it directly closes the loop-vs-classroom-loop conflation the "expert never left the loop" sentence creates; the second is lighter but leaves the loop-framing ambiguity in place. PI-099 status: open → open (re-confirmed at narrowed scope; XIV.C live-teaching-mention leg satisfied per run-254 Polish PI-170 closure, "loop"-framing-elides-classroom-judgment leg still open). Routes Section 14 polish_status:ready_for_stitch → needs_polish (already routed by the Persona 4 Provenance audit return this tick; no double-routing). No new PI opened.

### Persona 3: AI-in-Education Researcher
- [P1] Section XIV.C paragraph 1 sentences "The natural next step is a second instance in another course, one that would test the generalization the article advances and measure what its evaluation plan describes." in `manuscript/full-draft.md` line 405 and `manuscript/polished/section-14-conclusion.md` XIV.C paragraph 1 — opens PI-227, a new finding on a Bond-meta-review-collaboration-prong elision in the conclusion's future-work framing. The Bond meta-review's three-part call is "ethics, collaboration, and rigour" (the rigour anchor is canonical at four article sites: full-draft.md lines 411, 579, 695, 727 per the Stitch consistency-check log); the article's Section XI evaluation plan adopts the rigour standard explicitly and Section XII.E closes on collaboration as the prong the single-author case study can least claim, naming it as the named limit of the article's reach. XIV.C's "second instance in another course … that would test the generalization the article advances and measure what its evaluation plan describes" is the natural conclusion-level site to echo the collaboration prong because (a) it names the future-work direction at the place a JLE-type article positions its research-program contribution, (b) the [^182] *See supra* Sections X and XI cross-reference resolves to the two sections that develop the generalization and evaluation framing the second instance would apply, and (c) the article's own Section XII.E closing names collaboration as the limit, which makes the conclusion's silence on collaboration as a future-work axis read as the article not picking up its own thread. A learning-sciences reviewer reading XIV.C sees the future-work framing implying a single-faculty-member, single-institution n=2 replication — "a second instance in another course" reads naturally as a same-context-different-doctrine replication rather than as a cross-institutional collaborative study. The Bond-meta-review-collaboration anchor the article carries at full-draft.md line 727 (Section XII.E closing) and the institution-collaboration framing in Section XIII.B (library / instructional-design / IT stewardship) both establish collaboration as a load-bearing axis the conclusion should echo. The defect is the article's own future-work paragraph silently replicating the n=1 problem at n=2 by not naming the cross-institutional collaborative form that would address the structural limit XII.E identifies. Distinct from PI-100 (equity-and-accessibility-gap, the XIV silence on access/equity/digital-divide) and from PI-098 (replicability-overclaim, the XIV.A flat "can follow" framing); this is a finding on the conclusion's future-work paragraph omitting the Bond-meta-review collaboration prong the article elsewhere foregrounds.
  Suggested fix: in the next Polish or Cite tick on Section 14, expand XIV.C's "second instance" sentence to name the collaboration prong explicitly. Two forms work: (a) Rewrite the sentence to specify collaborative form: "The natural next step is a second instance in another course built and reviewed by a different faculty member at a different institution under a shared evaluation protocol, addressing the collaboration prong Bond's meta-review identifies as a field-level gap and that Section XII.E names as a structural limit of the single-author case study." (50 words, +25 from current; names cross-institutional collaboration as the future-work axis and anchors it to Bond's call). (b) Split into two sentences and add the cross-reference: "The natural next step is a second instance in another course, one that would test the generalization the article advances and measure what its evaluation plan describes. The form that most addresses Section XII.E's collaboration-as-limit observation and Bond's meta-review collaboration prong is a cross-institutional study with a different faculty member working under a shared evaluation protocol." (54 words, +29 from current; cleaner separation between the immediate next step and the field-level collaboration ambition). The (a) form is preferred because it bundles the collaboration anchor into the single second-instance sentence and keeps XIV.C compact; (b) is the lighter rewrite if the section's word budget cannot absorb (a)'s addition. PI-227 status: open (new). Routes Section 14 polish_status: ready_for_stitch → needs_polish (already routed by the Persona 4 Provenance audit return this tick; no double-routing). New PI opened: PI-227 type bond-collaboration-prong-elision-in-future-work-framing.

### Persona 5: Practitioner-Adopter [SUPPLEMENTAL — rotating in this tick per PERSONAS.md run_count-mod-3 = 285 mod 3 = 0]
- [P1] Section XIV.C paragraph 1 sentences "The article closes on the method-first, opt-in invitation that the institutional discussion handed forward. A faculty member adopts the method, namely the typed schema, the verified-source discipline, the professorial gating, and the iterative loop, rather than the constitutional-law artifact.[^181]" in `manuscript/full-draft.md` line 405 and `manuscript/polished/section-14-conclusion.md` XIV.C paragraph 1 — opens PI-229, a new finding on an adopter-first-step method-spec routing gap in XIV.C's [^181] anchor. The conclusion's [^181] footnote forwards adopters to Section XIII (Institutional Implications) for the method-first opt-in invitation context. A practitioner-adopter reading XIV.C as the article's "what now?" surface is given a method-spec — four named elements — and a footnote that resolves to the institutional-implications discussion rather than to the spec sections where each element is documented. The four named method elements have specific section anchors documented in the article: the typed schema is documented in Section V (Building the Obsidian Vault); the verified-source discipline is documented in Section VI (Prompting as Pedagogical Design) and developed further in Section VII (Iterative Improvement) and the *Prize Cases* narrative; the professorial gating is documented in Section VII; the iterative loop is documented in Section VII (with the six-phase rotation pinned to VII.B per the run-252 Cite PI-172 closure). An adopter following [^181] to Section XIII arrives at the institutional-implications discussion (who reviews each page, who maintains the vault, who is accountable for the public artifact) rather than at the spec sections that would let them start building. The defect is the conclusion's adoption-invitation routes adopters to the contextual section (Section XIII) rather than to the four spec sections (V, VI, VII) where the adoptable elements are documented for replication. Reading the Practitioner-Adopter Persona's PERSONAS.md mandate ("any place the method is NARRATED rather than SPECIFIED: missing steps, tools, decisions, or thresholds an adopter would need"), XIV.C names the method elements but routes their specifications backward to the institutional-implications context rather than to the spec sections; an adopter reading the conclusion as the article's adoption-onramp surface gets pointed at the wrong place. Distinct from PI-098 (replicability-overclaim, the flat "can follow" framing at XIV.A and the "modest, replicable infrastructure choice" framing at XIV.C closing); this is a routing finding on which sections the [^181] adoption-invitation footnote should forward to, not a finding on the replicability claim's strength.
  Suggested fix: in the next Polish or Cite tick on Section 14, expand [^181] from a single *See supra* Section XIII pointer to a compound cross-reference that routes adopters to BOTH the institutional context (Section XIII) AND the four spec sections where each named method element is documented. Example expanded [^181]: "*See supra* Section XIII (Institutional Implications) (the method-first, opt-in adoption invitation, under which a faculty member adopts the typed schema, the verified-source discipline, the professorial gating, and the iterative loop rather than the constitutional-law artifact); *see also supra* Section V (Building the Obsidian Vault) (specifying the typed schema for replication: Topic page, Case brief, Lecture summary schemas with their frontmatter conventions); *see also supra* Section VI (Prompting as Pedagogical Design) (specifying the verified-source discipline including the Midpage line-anchor convention and the build-time linking to indexed opinions); *see also supra* Section VII (Iterative Improvement Under Professorial Control) (specifying the professorial gating and the iterative loop: the six-phase rotation per VII.B, the forced-finding Verify pass per VII.C, the loop-memory discipline per VII.D)." (footnote word count ~120, somewhat above the L-059 50-word cap; alternative form is a leaner four-target pin-cite without the parenthetical descriptions). Alternatively, rewrite XIV.C's "A faculty member adopts the method, namely the typed schema, the verified-source discipline, the professorial gating, and the iterative loop" sentence to inline the section anchors: "A faculty member adopts the method — the typed schema (Section V), the verified-source discipline (Section VI), the professorial gating (Section VII), and the iterative loop (Section VII per VII.B's six-phase enumeration) — rather than the constitutional-law artifact." (inline form, +18 body words; routes adopters to the spec sections at the point of naming rather than through the footnote expansion). The inline form is preferred because it carries the routing at the body-prose level where an adopter encounters the method-element names; the footnote-expansion form is the L-059-word-cap-compliant alternative if the inline addition is judged to bloat XIV.C. PI-229 status: open (new). Routes Section 14 polish_status: ready_for_stitch → needs_polish (already routed by the Persona 4 Provenance audit return this tick; no double-routing).
